| ciatrklib ModulePositionJump (ciatrklib.c: `modulepositionjump_c`) | stores n in `mt_SongPos` and sets the jump flag; the next tick's `mt_NextPosition` increments it (effect Bxx stores xx - 1 for that reason): the song continues at position n + 1, a jump to the last position restarts the song | stores n - 1, as Bxx does: the song continues at position n | **F** | #447: every call (library test `rwe_ciatrklib`, `GetModulePosition|d0||2`: 3, and the play routine's state of the ticks after it) |
| ciatrklib TrackerVolume (ciatrklib.c: `volume_control_c`) | stores the new master volume, but applies it to the notes that play (`Change_Volume_Levels`) only if 0 < volume < 64: `TrackerVolume 0` does not silence them (a fade-out to 0 ends audible), `TrackerVolume 64` does not bring them back to full volume, until their volume is written again | every volume 0-64 is applied to the notes that play | **F** | #452: `TrackerVolume 0` and `64` while notes play (library test `rwe_ciatrklib`: the volume difference in the library's variables after `TrackerVolume $0`/`$40`, cases `...|modtable|...`; Paula's volume registers cannot be read) |
| ciatrklib ResetCIAInt (ciatrklib.c: `resetciaint`) | selects the bit for `RemICRVector` (`MOVEQ #1,D0` for timer B, `MOVEQ #0,D0` for timer A), but the common code at `RemInt` starts with another `MOVEQ #0,d0`: the vector is always removed with bit 0 (for timer B of ciab the library's vector stays installed and the one of ciab's timer A is removed) | removes the vector with the bit of its timer | **F** | #448: CIA mode on timer B of ciab: `ChangeTrackerVBlank`, `StartTracker`, the end of the program (library test `rwe_ciatrklib`, `ChangeTrackerVBlank|RemICRVector bit||1` and `||2`) |
| ciatrklib SetCIAInt (ciatrklib.c: `setciaint`) | loads the interrupt bit for `AddICRVector` with `MOVE.l %00000001,D0` (timer A of ciaa) and `MOVE.l %00000010,D0` (timer B of ciab), without `#`: the longs at the **addresses** 1 and 2 - an address error at the program's start on a 68000/68010, an invalid bit with ExecBase above 16 MB (the interrupt is accepted, but never plays: in CIA mode, the default, nothing plays) | the bits 0 (`CIAICRB_TA`) and 1 (`CIAICRB_TB`) | **F** | #450: every program that uses the library in CIA mode (test `ciatrklib-setciaint-icr-bit`: the module plays in Amiberry; library test `rwe_ciatrklib`, `init (CIA)|AddICRVector bit||...`) |
| ciatrklib FreeTrackerModule error check (ciatrklib.c: `freecheck_c`) | `_slotcheck` checks only the slot number: the module that plays is freed (also by `LoadTrackerModule`'s implicit free, which `_loadcheck` already refuses), and the play routine goes on reading the freed memory, also with runtime error checking | with runtime error checking, freeing the loaded or copied module that plays is the runtime error "Tracker: Stop the module before freeing it." (the new check `_freecheck`); without it the module is freed as before (a programming error, documented as unpredictable) | **F** | #436: only with runtime error checking (test `ciatrklib-free-while-playing`) |
| ciatrklib E8x Karplus-Strong (ciatrklib.c: `karplusstrong`) | averages the bytes of the voice's loop with the `DBRA` counter loop length × 2 − 2: for a loop length of 0 the counter is $FFFE and 65535 bytes from the loop start are rewritten; a voice that played no sample yet has loop start 0: E8x there rewrites the memory from address 0 (exception vectors, the ExecBase pointer), a crash | without a loop (length 0) E8x does nothing | **F** | #439: E8x on a voice without a loop (test `ciatrklib-karplus-loop0`); E8x on a loop is unchanged; a test of the loop length on E8x only (no cost on other notes or ticks) |
| ciatrklib tracker event 8xx (ciatrklib.c: `checkmoreefx`) | the event (read by GetTrackerEvent, CheckTrackerEvent, WaitTrackerEvent) is stored only by `mt_CheckEfx`, the effects of the ticks between the rows; `mt_CheckMoreEfx`, the row's own tick, has no case for 8: at speed 1 no event is ever seen (`WaitTrackerEvent` waits forever), at other speeds it comes one tick after its row | the event is also stored on the row's tick | **F** | #435: modules with 8xx: the event comes on its row's tick (speed 1: at all) (library test `rwe_ciatrklib`: the play routine's state of the ticks with an event, `WaitTrackerEvent (CIA)|WaitTOF calls`). The dispatch of the row's effects costs no cycle more (`SUBQ.B #8` instead of `ADDI.B #-9`: 4 cycles less) |
| ciatrklib mt_init sample clearing (ciatrklib.c: `mt_init`) | the library changed Protracker's `mt_init`, which clears the first long of every sample, so that it does not write behind the module, but tests `CMP.l a2,a4` with a4 = the slot's **length**, not the module's end: a length is far smaller than a sample address (a decoded module has the length 1), so the first long is practically never cleared, and every sample without a loop ends in a loop of its first two bytes (a DC level or a buzz) | the first long of every sample that lies inside the module is cleared (the end: the slot's address + length; for a module `DecodeModule` left in place, the end of its samples) | **F** | #438: every module with samples without a loop whose first two bytes are not 0: they end in silence, as in Protracker; `StartTracker` changes the module's memory there (test `ciatrklib-init-length-vs-address`, a loaded module; library test `rwe_ciatrklib`, a decoded one,, `StartTracker|module bytes|slot 0`, `...|module unchanged|slot 0`) |
| popupwinlib PopUpWindow, CalcWinSize (popupwinlib.c:453) | sizing a menu level that has an item with a sub menu ends with `BCHG #1,$bfe001` (a debug leftover): every such level toggles bit 1 of CIA-A PRA, the audio low-pass filter and the power LED | the bit stays as it is | **F** | #468: the filter/LED state after a popup with sub menus (test `popupwinlib-bchg-bfe001`, `libs_emulator` cases `[fixed #468]` of `rwe_popupwinlib`) |
| rimisclib InitArgParse (rimisclib.c:656) | copies the string with its 0 into its 256 byte buffer without a limit and clears four bytes behind the copy (an end guard for NextArg$, which stepped behind the 0): a string of more than 251 characters writes behind the buffer, into the code of NextArgChar$ and NextArg$ (a crash or a wrong result) | copies at most 255 characters and the 0; the rest of a longer string is cut off; no zeros behind the copy (NextArg$ stays on the 0, #755) | **F** | #752: only strings of more than 251 characters; up to 255 characters the same words, a longer one is parsed as its first 255 characters (test `rimisclib-initargparse-unbounded`) |
| rimisclib NextArg$ (rimisclib.c:681) | inside a quoted word the quotes are counted and a quote at an even count ends the word unless a quote follows, which is copied as a literal quote; the pair `""` moves the count on by one only, so the closing quote behind it copies the next character and the word goes on: `"a""b" c` is the one word `a"b c`, and at the end of the string the copied character is the 0 (the word gets it, the parse position goes behind it); the end of the string leaves the parse position behind the 0 | `""` inside quotes is one quote, a quote not followed by a quote ends the word: `a"b`, then `c`; the parse position stays on the 0 at the end | **F** | #755: only quoted words with `""` inside (test `rimisclib-nextarg-doubled-quote`, `libs_emulator` cases `[fixed #755]` of `rwe_rimisclib`); otherwise the same words |

| risoundlib, risoundlib2 CludgeSound (risoundlib.c:113, risoundlib2.c:618) | frees the old sample of the sound object (memlib globfree) without looking at audiolib's "cL" mark (+14): after a `DecodeSound` of an 8SVX file in chip memory the sample is the file's own BODY, which memlib never allocated; globfree does not find it and raises "Unable to Free Memory" (TRAP #0: the debugger's requester, without it a Guru) | the old sample is not freed when the mark is set | **F** | #945: only `CludgeSound` on a sound object filled by `DecodeSound` of chip data; it returns -1 and the program goes on (tests `risoundlib-cludgesound-frees-decoded`, `risoundlib2-cludgesound-frees-decoded`) |
| risoundlib2 CludgeSound (risoundlib2.c:618) | fills the sound object with a copy of the sample but no longer clears +14 (risoundlib's `MOVE.w #0,14(a0)` became the write of the rate at +16, #926): audiolib's "cL" mark of `DecodeSound` data stays, and audiolib never frees the copy (until memlib frees it at the end) | +14 is cleared, as in risoundlib | **F** | #925: only `CludgeSound` on a sound object marked "cL" (after a `DecodeSound` of chip data); the copy is freed again (test `risoundlib2-cludgesound-cl-mark-kept`, `libs_emulator` cases `[fixed #925]` of `rwe_risoundlib2`) |
| rimisclib ProgPath$, ProgDir$, ProgName$, DefaultFontName copyout (rimisclib.c:380, :1022) | copy the result with its 0 and leave a3 behind the 0: in a string expression the next part is written one byte too far (`ProgDir$+"/data"` holds the 0 and loses the "a") | a3 ends on the 0 | **F** | #743: only these functions followed by more parts of a string expression (test `rimisclib-progpath-a3-behind-0`, `libs_emulator` cases `[fixed #743]` of `rwe_rimisclib`) |

| rireqlib ReqFileRequest (rireqlib.c:222) | copies the path with its 0 and leaves a3 behind the 0: in a string expression the next part is written one byte too far | a3 ends on the 0 (on cancel unchanged, as before) | **F** | #784: only a confirmed requester followed by more parts of a string expression (`libs_emulator` cases `[fixed #784]` of `rwe_rireqlib`, fake req.library: a3 one byte lower) |

| ciatrklib, ritrackerlib mt_LowMask (ciatrklib.c: `playdata`, ritrackerlib.c: `pt`) | the Protracker 2.3A play routine's `mt_LowMask` (the parameter mask of 1xx/2xx, $0F for E1x/E2x) starts as `Dc.b 0`: the first 1xx or 2xx a program plays before any E1x/E2x slides by 0 on its first tick, and the note stays one step off for the rest of that portamento | starts as $FF | **F** | #478; observable: the period of the first 1xx/2xx (library tests `rwe_ciatrklib`, case `mt_LowMask 0` and the first ticks of the play routine; `rwe_ritrackerlib`, the ticks of the row with the first 1xx) |

| ciatrklib, ritrackerlib mt_SampleOffset (ciatrklib.c: `sampleoffset`, ritrackerlib.c: `mt_sampleoffset`) | compares the offset (xx x 128 words) with the voice's length signed (`CMP.W n_length(A6),D0`, `BGE`): a sample of 32768 words (64 KB) or more counts as shorter than every offset, also 900 - the length becomes 1 and the start stays | compared unsigned | **F** | #472; observable: 9xx on a sample of 64 KB or more (Protracker allows 128 KB) plays from the offset instead of 2 bytes (bug-orig tests `pt23a-9xx-signed-length`, `pt23a-9xx-signed-length-ciatrklib`) |
| ciatrklib, ritrackerlib mt_PlayVoice, loop start (ciatrklib.c: `playvoice`, ritrackerlib.c: `mt_playvoice`) | the loop start is `n_start` + 2 x repeat with `ASL.W #1,D3`: the doubling is a word operation, so from a repeat of 32768 words on bit 16 is lost and the loop starts 64 KB too early (EFx then inverts the wrong bytes) | doubled as a long | **F** | #479; observable: a sample longer than 64 KB whose loop starts at 64 KB or later (bug-orig tests `pt23a-repeat-word`, `pt23a-repeat-word-ciatrklib`) |
| ciatrklib, ritrackerlib mt_Arpeggio (ciatrklib.c: `arpeggio`, ritrackerlib.c: `mt_arpeggio`) | finds the voice's period in its finetune's row with 37 comparisons and plays the entry x (or y) half-tones higher without a limit: for a note near the top of the row the entry lies behind it - the next finetune's row (a low note), for finetune -1 the words behind the table (voice 1's data) | 36 comparisons; the entry at most the row's last (index 35) | **F** | #474; observable: an arpeggio on a high note (e.g. B-3 with 047) (bug-orig test `pt23a-arpeggio-overrun-ciatrklib`; library tests `rwe_ritrackerlib`, rows by hand, and `rwe_ciatrklib`, random arpeggios on masked voices) |
| ciatrklib, ritrackerlib mt_end after F00 (ciatrklib.c: `mt_end`, ritrackerlib.c: `mt_end`) | F00 (`mt_SetSpeed`: `BEQ mt_end`) is processed while the row is read; `mt_end` loads a0 with `$DFF000` for its volume writes, but a0 is `mt_GetNewNote`'s pattern pointer: the voices behind an F00 on voice 1-3 read their notes from the custom chip registers (`$DFF000` + pattern offset) - random periods, samples and effects, whose DMA `mt_SetDMA` switches on again | `mt_end` leaves the pattern pointer alone: the voices behind read their notes from the pattern | **F** | #475; observable: F00 on voice 1-3 (library tests `rwe_ciatrklib`, the F00 phase, and `rwe_ritrackerlib`, the second module's F00 on voice 1: the redirected registers are filled with notes) |
| ciatrklib, ritrackerlib mt_init, highest pattern (ciatrklib.c: `maxpattern`, ritrackerlib.c: `mt_init`) | the search for the highest pattern number decrements its counter once more for a new maximum (SUBQ, the DBRA skipped): positions 0-126 are read, 127 never; a new maximum at position 126 turns the counter 0 into $FFFF and the search reads 65535 bytes more (ritrackerlib then clears the first long of 31 "samples" far behind the module; ciatrklib's DecodeModule copies up to 128 KB too much to chip memory); the numbers are compared signed | all 128 positions, one DBRA per byte, compared unsigned | **F** | #477 (data from outside, load time: TrackInit, StartTracker, DecodeModule); observable: a module whose highest pattern is first used at position 126 or only at 127 (bug-orig tests `pt23a-init-pattern-scan`, `pt23a-init-pattern-scan-ciatrklib`; library test `rwe_ritrackerlib`, pattern 1 at position 127) |
| ciatrklib, ritrackerlib sample numbers above 31 (ciatrklib.c: `clearsamplenums`, ritrackerlib.c: `mt_init`) | `mt_PlayVoice` uses a note's sample number (1-255) without a check: as an index into `mt_SampleStarts` (31 set) and as the offset of the sample header; sample 32 starts at 0, 33 at the module itself, higher ones at the play routine's variables, with length, finetune, volume and loop from the position table and the patterns | `mt_init` sets the sample number of every note above 31 to 0 in the patterns, once (the module is changed in memory; ciatrklib: inside the slot's length when it is known) - no test per note | **F** | #480 (data from outside: defective modules or other formats); load time about 50 cycles per note (64 patterns: about 120 ms on a 68000, as trackerlib's #295); observable: a note with a sample number of 32 or more keeps the voice's sample (bug-orig tests `pt23a-samplenum-over-31`, `pt23a-samplenum-over-31-ciatrklib`) |
| ciatrklib, ritrackerlib finetune byte above 15 (ciatrklib.c: `maskfinetunes`, ritrackerlib.c: `mt_init`) | `mt_PlayVoice` copies byte 24 of the sample header unmasked into `n_finetune`; every period lookup multiplies it by 72: a value up to 255 reads up to 18 KB behind the 1152-byte period table (the voices, the variables, the library's code) as periods | `mt_init` masks the finetune bytes of the 31 headers to 0-15, once (the module is changed in memory) - no AND per note | **F** | #476 (data from outside: defective modules or other formats); observable: the pitch of a sample with such a byte (bug-orig tests `pt23a-finetune-above-15`, `pt23a-finetune-above-15-ciatrklib`) |

| ritrackerlib SetTempo (ritrackerlib.c: `settempo`) | a tempo command (F20-F7F, with #569 F20-FFF) writes `deftime` / tempo to `ciatime` and to the latch of CIA-B timer A (`$BFD400`/`$BFD500`); the library never allocates that timer (no `ciab.resource` call), and `deftime` is set only by the dead `_ciaplayerinit` (an `RTS` first), so it writes 0: another user of the timer gets its period changed, and the write of the high byte loads the counter of a stopped timer (and starts it in one-shot mode) | stores only `RealTempo`; the program plays the module with `TrackPlay`, so the tempo does not change the speed (as before) | **F** | #571; observable: CIA-B timer A after a tempo command (library test `rwe_ritrackerlib`, the timer's counter after the second module's F20 and F7D) |
| ritrackerlib mt_init sample clearing (ritrackerlib.c: `mt_init`) | clears the first long of every sample (`mtloop3: CLR.L (A2)`), whatever its length: an empty sample at the end of the module starts at the module's end, so the 4 bytes behind the module are cleared (2 behind it for a last sample of one word) - the next variable or a memory header behind a module in a block of its size | only if the long lies before the end of the samples (the sum of the 31 lengths), as ciatrklib's `mt_init` for a module of unknown length; TrackInit has no module length, so a module shorter than its header claims (defective input) is still written behind | **F** | #570; observable: the bytes behind a module whose last samples are empty (library test `rwe_ritrackerlib`, the 16 guard bytes behind the first module) |
| ritrackerlib Tracker (ritrackerlib.c: `tracker_c`) | `Tracker Off` calls `mt_end`, which clears `mt_Enable`; `Tracker On` only ANDs `mt_Enable` with its value (`AND.b d0,mt_Enable`): after `Tracker Off`, `Tracker On` does not continue the module, which stays stopped until `TrackInit` or `TrackerPause 0` | `Tracker Off` keeps `mt_Enable` (the voices are silenced as before, `TrackPlay` does nothing while off); `Tracker On` continues a module that was playing, one stopped or paused before stays stopped | **F** | #572; observable: `Tracker Off` followed by `Tracker On` (library test `rwe_ritrackerlib`, the ticks "Tracker On after Off") |
| ritrackerlib TrackInit while Tracker Off (ritrackerlib.c: `mt_init`) | `mt_init` returns at once while the status is off (`TST.b status: BNE 'ok: RTS`), but `_initmodule` still enables playing (`ST mt_Enable`): `Tracker On` then plays the previous module from its old position, or - without an earlier `TrackInit` - reads a "module" from address 0 | the song is initialised in any case; only the filter bit and `mt_end` (hardware writes) are left out while off | **F** | #573; observable: `Tracker Off`, `TrackInit`, `Tracker On`, `TrackPlay` (library test `rwe_ritrackerlib`, `TrackInit (Tracker Off)` and the ticks "Tracker 1: old song", which now play the second module) |
| ritrackerlib TrackerChannels, masked voices (ritrackerlib.c: `trackermask_c`, `mt_music_c`) | the mask is tested only for the new notes of a row; `_cut` zeroes pointer, length, period and volume of a masked voice but leaves its audio DMA on, and on every other tick `mt_NoNewAllChannels` runs the effects of all four voices on Paula (period every tick, the volume of Axy/7xy), and `mt_SetDMA` writes the loop pointer and length of all four after every row: the masked voice can become audible again with its last sample's loop | the masked voice's DMA is switched off, and the play routine writes its registers into a block nobody reads; the voices' register addresses come from a table (cheaper per voice than the computed address) | **F** | #567; observable: a voice masked while its note had an effect, or after the next row (library test `rwe_ritrackerlib`, the redirected registers with the masks $0B and $06) |
| ritrackerlib TrackerChannels, pattern offset (ritrackerlib.c: `mt_music_c`, `trackermask_c`) | `mt_GetNewNote` skips `BSR mt_PlayVoice` for a masked voice (`BTST #n,d2: BEQ`), and the step to the next voice's note (`ADDQ.L #4,D1`) is inside `mt_PlayVoice`: the voices after a masked one play the notes of the voice before (samples, periods, effects), the last voice's notes are not played at all | a masked voice plays its notes silently (`n_dmabit` 0, its registers in the block nobody reads), so its speed, jump and break effects apply as with all four voices; the mask test per voice is gone (cheaper per row) | **F** | #568; observable: `TrackerChannels` with a cleared bit followed by a voice that plays (library test `rwe_ritrackerlib`, the masks $0B and $06) |
| bb_blitlib BTBlockImage (bb_blitlib.c: `btblockimage_c`) | adds image × 224 to `image_addr` as a word (`MULU.w #SHAPE_BYTESIZE,d4 : ADD.w d4,a3`, sign extended): images 147 to 292 are read 64 KB lower, before `image_addr`, from image 293 on the offset wraps around | the offset is added as a long, as `TBlockImage` does; once per call, nothing added per plane | **F** | #427; observable with image tables larger than 32 KB (test `bb_blitlib-btblockimage-word-offset`: image 150 is drawn, the original drew the zeros 31936 bytes before `image_addr`) |
| bb_blitlib BPredatorBlit (bb_blitlib.c: `bpredatorblit_c`) | the offset and the modulos use the bitmap's bytes per row, but the B channel (the line below) adds `SetupMapTwo`'s width (`ADD.l ebwidth-2(pc),d1`): on a bitmap of another width B reads the wrong part of the background | B adds the bitmap's bytes per row; loaded once per call, kept in a register (per plane cheaper than the load of `ebwidth`) | **F** | #426; observable only on a bitmap whose width is not `SetupMapTwo`'s (test `bb_blitlib-bpredatorblit-b-ebwidth`: a 640-pixel bitmap without `SetupMapTwo`, pixel 100,10 gets the line below's colour 1, the original the colour 320 pixels to the right, 0) |
| bb_blitlib CreateShape error check (bb_blitlib.c: `errcreateshape_c`) | `CreateShape` has no error check (`!subs {_createshape,0,0}`) and counts with `SUBQ.w #1,d1 … DBRA`: a count of 0 creates 65536 shape objects, 2 MB written over the memory behind the shape table, also with runtime error checking | the command unchanged (an invalid argument); a new error check (debugger only) refuses a count of 0 with "CreateShape count must be at least 1!" | **F** | #428; observable with runtime error checking (test `bb_blitlib-createshape-count0`: the original crashes or hangs). The declaration differs from the reference (`bb_blitlib.declarations-intended.txt`) |
| bb_blitlib depth 0 error checks (bb_blitlib.c: `errshapea1_c`, `errshapea3_c`, `errsblocktoshape_c`) | `SpecialBlock`, `SpecialBlockBitmap`, `TBlock`, `PredatorBlit`, `BPredatorBlit` and `BColourBlit` have no error check, `SpecialBlockToShape`'s tests only the destination and the sizes; the plane loops count with `SUBQ.w #1 … DBRA` on the shape's depth: a shape of depth 0 (never defined) blits 65536 planes through the "plane pointers" behind the bitmap object, also with runtime error checking | the commands unchanged (an invalid argument; no test per blit on the hot path); new error checks (debugger only) for the eight command forms and a depth test in `SpecialBlockToShape`'s check refuse a shape of depth 0 with "Uninitialized Shape". A bitmap of depth 0 (the save loops) cannot be made with runtime error checking (bitmaplib's `_bitmapchk`, `cludgechk`) | **F** | #429; observable with runtime error checking (test `bb_blitlib-depth0-dbra`: `TBlock` of a shape of depth 0, the original blits 65536 times). The declarations differ from the reference (`bb_blitlib.declarations-intended.txt`) |
| bb_blitlib BlockToShape (bb_blitlib.c: `blocktoshape_c`, `errblocktoshape_c`) | one blit of height × depth lines of one word (`ASL.w #6,d0 : ADDQ.w #1,d0`), whatever the shape's width: of a shape wider than 16 pixels only the first 2/`ebwidth` of the data is copied, the rest of the destination keeps its old content; the error check tests only the destination and the sizes, so a shape of more than 1024 lines (height × depth) blits the size word's low bits | the blit is `ebwidth`/2 words wide (64 words as 0); the error check (debugger only) also refuses more than 1024 lines or a width above 64 words with "Shape too large for BlockToShape!". The entry is the C function (`BLITZ_ALIAS`, no thunk): it changes d0/d1 as the original; about 344 68000 cycles per call (original 340, the thunked port before 434) | **F** | #425; observable for shapes wider than 16 pixels (test `bb_blitlib-blocktoshape-one-word`: a 32×16×4 shape, colour 15 copied, the original 3) and, with runtime error checking, for a shape of 1280 lines (test `bb_blitlib-blocktoshape-too-large`) |

## 4. deflibs (AmigaOS call declarations)

| Library/routine (file:line) | Original | C version | Kind | Reason / observable? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| init of all libraries/resources (libs/deflibs/deflib.h:16) | `BEQ init+6` jumps into the middle of the `LEA`: `ORI.b #0,(a0)+` with arbitrary a0, then OpenLibrary with the a1 left by the failure, forever | the base or 0 (no retry); the finit of a library closes it only if the base is not 0 | **F** | #44: a program using a call of a missing library no longer hangs at startup; it runs with base 0, a call of that library crashes (test `deflibs-init-endless-retry`, Kickstart 1.3 without gadtools.library) |
| init/finit in general ([deflibs.md](deflibs.md)) | destroys a6, `JMP CloseLibrary` | GCC saves a6, `JSR` | **B** (register preservation) | not observable |
| Resources (deflib.h, e.g. battclocklib.c:42) | `LEA` points 6 bytes into the name: `OpenResource` always fails, the init hangs (#44) | opens the name itself | **F** | #45: a program using a call of ciaa, ciab, disk, misc, potgo, battclock, battmem or card no longer hangs at startup (test `deflibs-resource-name-skew`) |
| exec `CachePreDMA_`, `CachePostDMA_` (execlib.c:141) | `flags` declared in d1, exec reads d0 | d0 (`exec_lib.fd`); a declaration difference, listed in `libs/deflibs/declarations-intended.txt` | **F** | #959: exec gets the given flags; before, only by chance at the statement level (the flags evaluated last into d0), inside an expression d0 held another value (test `deflibs-cachedma-flags-d1`) |

## 5. Compiler blitz2-c (`programs/blitz2`)

Produces byte-identical programs (test corpus and CD reference run), hence only
internal deviations; except the fixed original bugs (kind **F**), which
change the generated code of the programs they concern. Conventions: `-fbaserel` (globals a4-relative,
`GLUE_LOAD_A4` in all entry points from outside, [README](../blitz2/bb2-readme.md)), assembler only in
`programs/blitz2/src/glue*.c`, registers with persistent state as globals
(`srcptr`; `numval`/`numfloat` local to libcall.c).

| Place (file:line) | Original | C version | Kind | observable? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| errstack/errcont (asmlibs.c:1427, compile.c:184, data.c:1460, data.c:1830, main.c:1354, parse.c:216, parse.c:305, stmts.c:1189, stmts.c:1634) | error return is not reset | `errjmp = olderr` | **T** | stack trick (a7/errcont) only with longjmp in C; no |
| Stack tricks `addq #4,a7` / `move.l passstack,a7` (asmlibs.c:403, :467, asmlibs.h:69, compile.c:1108, tokens1.c:840, tokens1.c:953) | return past the caller | return value / longjmp | **T** | no |
| Placeholder longs (compile.c:829, stmts.c:432, tokens1.c:256, :545, :673, :1158, tokens2.c:10) | poke register leftovers as placeholders | 0 or the recognisable value | **T** | are always overwritten (except nomemleft); no |
| Register results (eval.h:236, eval.c:126, libcall.h:19, compile.c:1385, tokens1.c:343, tokens2.c:81, parse.c:1163) | results in d2/d3/d6/CCR, values from the caller's stack | globals / parameters / return values | **T** | no |
| Constant folding (eval.h:171, eval.c:824, libcall.c:1182) | executes generated code with the compiler registers | `callregs` with the relevant registers | **T** | no |
| Range/overflow checks (eval.c:486, data.c:1693, parse.c:351, compile.c:198) | without check (reading/writing beyond tables, DIVU by 0) | C checks and does nothing | **K** | only in cases that according to the comment do not occur |

The individual reasons are each given at the comment `DIFF:` in
`programs/blitz2/src/`.

## 5a. Editor ted-c (TED 1.1, `programs/ted`)

The reference is the vasm build of `ted.s` ([`../ted/reference.md`](../ted/reference.md)).
Separate list of the TED deviations: [`../ted/deviations.md`](../ted/deviations.md)
(continuously extended there); here the rows that concern the shared
file requester.

No (except the double relocation, which never happens in the original) |

## 6. Error checks outside the error check area

A check function in the normal code is kept in every program, also
without the debugger (the behaviour is the same: the thunks in the check
area call it). Top-level assembler that switches the section is
invisible to GCC: a `BLITZ_ERRCHK` C function behind it can get no
section directive and land in the normal code. The assembler routines
of `blitzlib.h` (`BLITZ_ERRCHK_THUNK`, `BLITZ_ERRCHK_MESSAGE`,
`BLITZ_ERRCHK_AB`, the thunks of the normal code ...) are therefore C
functions whose body is the assembler text, and GCC switches the section
itself (#1056). So is the libraries' own assembler (`BLITZ_ERRCHK_ASM`,
`BLITZ_ASM_ROUTINE`, `BLITZ_ASM_BLOCK`), and the build step of every
library refuses a section directive in the assembler of its sources
(#1057). `blitzlib check` reports a check thunk in the error check area
whose C function is in the normal code, in the build step of every
library (`docs/development.md`, "Checks in the build"). Every
`BLITZ_ERRCHK` function and message of the acidlibs and the RWE userlibs
is in the error check area (checked against the symbols of all `.linked`
files for #1056).

## 7. State

All command libraries are ported (c4a736d); the tables also contain
scrolllib, 2dlib, polylib (S12) and gameiolib, blitzkeyslib, animlib,
blitzcoplib, displaylib (S13); dhandlerlib (lib 1), the last original
binary in acidlibs, followed on 2026-10-29.

## Summary

* **B – user decision:** the a2 convention (about 20 libraries,
  never observable) and register preservation "at least like the original"
  (additionally preserved registers are never results).
* **T – technically unavoidable:** undefined register leftovers and
  OS returns (blitlib BlitColl, freqlib, cliargslib CCR, windowslib
  zcursor, ilbmifflib a0), memory layout with overflows and
  invalid indices (2dlib, polylib, displaylib, crunchlib), stack contents
  with error jumps via JSR (displaylib) and other stack questions
  (crunchlib, arrayslib a4/a5), timing (shapetrixlib XFlip) and in the
  compiler all stack tricks and placeholders. Observable at most in
  error cases, with overflows or corrupted data.
* **K – silent corrections (existing):** traps in normal code instead of a jump
  into the cut-off error check area (inputoutputlib, fileiolib,
  palettelib, windowslib, brexxlib), return instead of crash
  or continuing error chain or running into the message text (arexxlib,
  memlib err1/locfree, runerrlib, fontlib, palettelib, seriallib noser,
  utillib CreateMsgPort), preserved a3 (linklistlib, medlib, seriallib,
  windowslib, blitzoslib), the title length not written into the tag list
  (windowslib WindowTags &TagList) and word coordinates
  (blitzoslib), in the compiler some range checks (pushprec, savefile).
  Observable mostly only in error cases without debugger or with a
  returning trap; visible in a normal run are seriallib
  ReadSerial in string expressions and WindowTags &TagList (memory behind
  the tag list).
* **F – fixed original bugs (intended):** the rows of kind **F** in the
  tables above, each with its issue and test; mostly error routines and
  their messages moved out of the error check area, overruns, leaks and
  wrong compares. They change the output of the affected cases on
  purpose.
